# Idea ledger, 2026-11-09

Every idea from the three idea rounds, copied mechanically from the agents' structured output by a script. Nothing here is a result. Claims and predictions are hypotheses. "Nearest work" is what the idea's author listed; in rounds 1 and 3 many of those searches were throttled, so treat a missing reference as "not searched", not as "open". Only the fourteen first-round ideas marked *rechecked* had a full search-backed check. Long fields are cut at a word boundary and end in an ellipsis. The reading of all this is in [the research directions document](2026-11-09-research-directions.md).

## Round 1: nine lenses, 71 ideas

### Lens: compression

**Temporal dithering: alternate two complementary 4-bit roundings across denoising steps** (image and video diffusion (flow-matching DiTs); self-rated novelty 4)  
Hypothesis: For samplers of 20 or more steps, alternating floor and ceil 4-bit roundings of one 5-bit code file from step to step gives a final latent at least 30% closer to the 5-bit model's result than a fixed nearest-rounded 4-bit model does, same seed and prompts.  
First experiment: M1 Max only, simulated quantization, no new kernel. Stage 1 (about an hour): Wan 2.1 1.3B in fp16; along the fp16 trajectory of 8 prompts, at each of 20 steps compute the velocity with fp16 weights and with rotated 4-bit weights at the same latent, and report the cosine between the velocity errors …  
Kill criterion: Stage 1 rho below 0.3 (errors at neighbouring steps are already unrelated, so under about 27% is available), or Stage 2 shows the alternating arm less than 30% closer to the 5-bit result than nearest-rounded 4-bit at both 20 and 50 steps.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14062](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14062), 2026-03 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18095](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18095), 2026-03 (read); [https://arxiv.org/html/2501.08180v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2501.08180v2), 2025-01 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20348](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20348), 2025-10 (not read directly)

**Variable-rate weights decoded inside the kernel, on the one platform where fixed-rate tiles do not bind** (weights of image and video DiTs; the method is …; self-rated novelty 4)  
Hypothesis: On an M1 Max, an entropy-coded scalar weight format decoded in the same Metal pass that already unpacks weights to fp16 matches INT8 ConvRot's weight error at 7.2 bits per weight or fewer and W4A8's at 4.1 bits or fewer, with linear-layer time within 10% of the fixed-rate kernels.  
First experiment: Stage 0, CPU only (Mac or a Modal CPU job in the style of the repo's weight-error app): measure the zeroth-order entropy of the existing INT8 ConvRot codes and W4A8 indexes for the repo's Qwen-Image 2.1 build and Wan 2.1 1.3B, per layer. My prediction is 7.0 to 7.5 bits for the INT8 codes. Add …  
Kill criterion: Stage 0 shows under 0.3 bit of entropy slack in the existing codes and water-filled allocation saves under 0.3 bit at equal activation-weighted error; or Stage 1 makes the linear layer more than 15% slower than the INT8 kernel for both designs, in which case …  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04956](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04956), 2026-03 (v2 2026-06) (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23406](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23406), 2026-06-22 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25188](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25188), 2026-08-25 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01384](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01384), 2024-03 (read)

**Siblings as conditional codes: store a model family once plus a low-rate difference** (video and image DiT families (Wan 2.2 high-noise and …; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: For at least two of the four sibling pairs, the second model can be coded given the first model's quantized weights at 1.5 bits per weight or fewer while keeping W4A8-level weight error (7.3%), against 4.5 bits when coded alone.  
First experiment: CPU only, weight-level, no GPU: stream each pair layer by layer (Mac, or a Modal CPU job like the repo's weight-error app). Pairs: Wan 2.2 T2V 14B high-noise against low-noise; Qwen-Image against Qwen-Image-Edit; Krea 2 Raw against Turbo; Qwen2.5-7B base against instruct as a control expected to be …  
Kill criterion: Relative difference above 50% in most layers of a pair means over 2.8 bits at 4-bit fidelity, a saving under 35%, not worth a kernel for that pair. If all three diffusion pairs exceed that, drop the idea for diffusion and keep the measurement as a short …  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16940](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16940), 2026-04-18 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00658](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00658), 2026-05-30 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.19720](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.19720), 2025-12 (not read directly)

**A perplexity for diffusion: held-out denoising loss as the cheap, low-noise quality axis for weight formats** (image and video diffusion; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: The rise in held-out flow-matching loss caused by a weight format, measured on about 200 real captioned images at 8 noise levels, ranks at least 8 formats in the same order as paired sampled-image metrics (Spearman 0.9 or higher) and grows linearly with squared weight error (R squared 0.95 or higher).  
First experiment: M1 Max. Models: Wan 2.1 1.3B on single frames or very short clips, plus one small image DiT such as PixArt-Sigma (0.6B). Cache VAE latents and text embeddings for 200 held-out captioned images once. Formats: fp16, INT8 ConvRot, W6A8, W4A8, Q8_0, Q6_K, Q5_K, Q4_K, Q3_K, plus Gaussian weight noise at …  
Kill criterion: Spearman below 0.7 against the sampled metrics and the blind A/B, or loss increases for formats at 6 bits and above that are inside run-to-run noise (the metric is blind exactly where format choices get made).  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.05431](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.05431), 2023-12 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.17525](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.17525), 2024-11 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12280](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12280), 2026-06-10 (read)

**White-error test: are rotation and low-rank correction substitutes, and does rotation make adapters portable?** (image and video DiT weights plus one LLM; LoRA training on …; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: At 4 bits, a rank-32 residual branch cuts activation-weighted output error by more than 20% on unrotated formats but by less than 5% once the weights are Hadamard-rotated as in the repo's formats.  
First experiment: Part A, CPU or Mac, no training: for the repo's Qwen-Image 2.1 build, Wan 2.1 1.3B and Llama-3.2-1B, compute the error matrix E per layer for an unrotated 4-bit format (Q4_K and an NF4-style codebook) and for rotated W4A8. Capture activation second moments from 16 prompts on the Mac. Report the …  
Kill criterion: If unrotated 4-bit error is already white (top-32 share under 1.5x the white share), the question is empty at 4 bits: report that and stop. If the rank-32 branch still cuts more than 15% of output error after rotation, the two are complements, the hypothesis …  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25188](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25188), 2026-08-25 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05007](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05007), 2024-11 (not read directly); [https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1332.pdf](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1332.pdf), 2026 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/html/2509.25214v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.25214v1), 2025-09 (not read directly)

**One noise-gain number per model: is weight SNR a sufficient statistic across modalities and sizes?** (cross-modal: LLM, image and video DiT, ASR, TTS; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: For rotated formats, real quantization and Gaussian weight noise of the same relative size raise the model's own loss by the same amount to within 15% in every modality tested, and the remaining per-model constant falls with parameter count along one power law within each model family.  
First experiment: Small models, free hardware. On the 8 GB GTX-class PC in fp32 (these cards gain nothing from fp16, so fp32 and models up to about 1.5B): Whisper tiny, base, small, medium and large-v3 (39M to 1.55B), word error rate and token loss on 200 LibriSpeech test-clean utterances; Qwen2.5 0.5B, 1.5B and 3B, …  
Kill criterion: Real rotated quantization and matched Gaussian noise differ by more than 2x in loss increase in two or more modalities (SNR is not sufficient), or the noise gain shows no size trend inside the Whisper and Qwen families (R squared below 0.5), which kills the …  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23406](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23406), 2026-06-22 (read); [https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.142](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.142), 2026 (read); [https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.17525](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.17525), 2024-11 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14302](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14302), 2025-05 (not read directly)

**Precision as the thing that gets paged: nested bit planes that degrade instead of swapping on small Macs** (image and video diffusion on unified-memory machines of 16 …; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: A single nested 8-bit weight file run with only its top 5 bit planes resident stays within W4A8's weight error (7.3%) at 5 bits per weight, and under memory pressure that forces the plain INT8 file to swap it finishes a generation at least 2x faster.  
First experiment: M1 Max, in three steps. (1) Weight-level table from the existing INT8 ConvRot codes of the repo's Qwen-Image 2.1 build: error at 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8 planes, with and without a half-step reconstruction offset. (2) Mask low planes in the existing decode (a small change) and run 20-step generations at 5, …  
Kill criterion: Plane-masked 5-plane error above 1.5x W4A8's on real layers, or Metal needs a private copy of mapped pages (then eviction frees nothing). In the second case drop the paging part and keep only the per-step schedule result.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03537](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03537), 2026-02 (v2 2026-09-30) (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16184](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16184), 2026-07-17 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06786](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06786), 2025-02 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14062](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14062), 2026-03 (read)

**How few weight bits does the backward pass need? A rate curve for LoRA gradients** (LoRA training on quantized image and video DiTs; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: In LoRA training on an INT8 base, running the frozen-weight backward product with only the top two bit planes of each weight gives adapter gradients with cosine 0.9 or higher to the full backward and the same held-out loss after 1,000 steps within seed spread, while sign-only weights do not.  
First experiment: M1 Max, using the repo's packed backward: add a mask in the backward decode that keeps the top k planes (k = 8, 4, 2, 1) with a matching rescale. (1) An afternoon: on the repo's Qwen-Image 2.1 INT8 build (9.46 GiB in the LoRA run), compute the cosine between LoRA A and B gradients from the masked …  
Kill criterion: Gradient cosine at k = 2 below 0.7 in the layers farthest from the loss, or the 2-plane runs lose by more than the seed-to-seed spread. Then the answer is that the backward needs the forward's bits; write it down and stop.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05925](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05925), 2025-02 (withdrawn 2026-07) (read); [https://crusoe.ai/resources/blog/quantized-weights-full-precision](https://crusoe.ai/resources/blog/quantized-weights-full-precision), 2026-10 (read); [https://icml.cc/virtual/2026/72410](https://icml.cc/virtual/2026/72410), 2026 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05711](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05711), 2026-07-07 (read)

### Lens: crossmodal

**Bias or noise? Step-to-step correlation of quantization error in diffusion sampling, and a one-anchor correction** (image and video diffusion (flow-matching transformers); self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: For a fixed prompt and seed, the error a quantized DiT adds to the velocity is mostly the same vector at every step (mean cosine above 0.5 between steps two or more apart), so measuring it once against full precision and subtracting it at the other steps removes at least 30% of the final-latent error.  
First experiment: Mac Studio, no cloud. Wan 2.1 1.3B (30 steps, small frame count) and one few-step model the repo already runs (Krea Turbo or Qwen-Image Lightning, 4-8 steps). Run the fp16 reference trajectory for 16 prompts x 4 seeds and save every x_t. Feed the same x_t to the W4A8 and INT8 ConvRot versions and …  
Kill criterion: Mean cosine between errors two or more steps apart is below 0.2 on both models, or the one-anchor correction cuts final-latent error by less than 15% relative, or a scalar PTQD-style correction does as well.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18095](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18095), 2026-03 (revised 2026-09) (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10657](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10657), 2023-05 (read); [https://icml.cc/virtual/2026/poster/64968](https://icml.cc/virtual/2026/poster/64968), 2026-07 (not read directly); [https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2026/html/Zeng_Sampling-Aware_Quantization_for_Diffusion_Models_CVPR_2026_paper.html](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2026/html/Zeng_Sampling-Aware_Quantization_for_Diffusion_Models_CVPR_2026_paper.html), 2026-06 (not read directly)

**P-frames for speech tokens: training-free layer reuse between adjacent frames in autoregressive TTS models** (speech synthesis (codec-token language models); self-rated novelty 4)  
Hypothesis: In autoregressive speech-token models, the update each middle layer writes to the residual stream at frame n has cosine similarity above 0.9 with the update at frame n-1, clearly higher than in the same backbone on text, so at least 30% of layer evaluations can be replaced by the previous frame's update with WER within 0.3 points absolute and speaker similarity within 0.01.  
First experiment: Mac Studio, PyTorch MPS, fp16. Llasa-1B (single codebook, 50 tokens/s) and CosyVoice 2 (0.5B backbone, 25 tokens/s), 100 sentences from Seed-TTS-eval. Step 1, measurement only: per layer, cosine between the block's residual update at frame n and n-1, with Qwen2.5-0.5B on plain text as the control. …  
Kill criterion: Adjacent-frame update similarity is below 0.8 in every layer, or is no higher than the text control, or no setting that skips at least 25% of layer evaluations keeps WER within +0.3 absolute and speaker similarity within -0.01.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/html/2603.09215](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.09215), 2026-03 (read); [https://arxiv.org/html/2606.09019v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.09019v1), 2026-06 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14709](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14709), 2025-05 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08696](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08696), 2025-09 (not read directly)

**Trellis-coded weights for diffusion transformers, judged with codec-style rate-distortion curves** (image and video diffusion; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: Trellis-coded weights at 3 bits per weight match the per-layer error of uniform 4-bit on Wan and Qwen-Image linears, and at 2048 or more tokens per call the decode adds under 10% to step time on an M1 Max, so both Wan 2.2 14B experts fit in memory at once at 4-bit quality.  
First experiment: Step 1, Modal CPU only (same pattern as the repo's weight-error job, a few dollars of the $30 credit): quantize the linear layers of Wan 2.1 1.3B and Qwen-Image with the public QTIP code at 2, 3 and 4 bits, data-free, and compare per-layer relative Frobenius error with the repo's existing numbers …  
Kill criterion: Trellis at 3 bits has higher per-layer error than uniform W4A8 on most layers, or the BD-rate gain over the repo's W4/W6 formats is under 10%, or decode overhead at M = 4096 on the M1 Max is above 15% of step time.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11235](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11235), 2024-06 (read); [docs/research/2026-10-25-exl3-prototype.md](2026-10-25-exl3-prototype.md), 2026-10-25 (read); [docs/research/2026-11-09-weight-error-by-format.md](2026-11-09-weight-error-by-format.md), 2026-11-09 (read)

**Delta-coded model families: one resident base plus 1-bit deltas for the Edit, Turbo and second-expert variants** (image and video diffusion; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: For at least one widely used pair (Wan 2.2 high-noise and low-noise experts, Qwen-Image and Qwen-Image-Edit, or a base and its distilled Turbo), a sign-only delta with one scale per row, added inside the matmul to an INT8 base, reproduces the second model's layer outputs with lower error than a separate 4-bit copy of that model.  
First experiment: Modal CPU only, a few dollars: load pairs of fp checkpoints and report per layer the relative delta norm, the error left after sign-plus-row-scale coding, and the delta's stable rank. Pairs: Wan 2.2 T2V 14B high-noise versus low-noise, Qwen-Image versus Qwen-Image-Edit, Krea Raw versus Turbo …  
Kill criterion: Relative delta norm is above 30% in most layers for every pair, or sign-plus-scale coding leaves more than half of the delta's energy and a 2-bit delta still loses to a separate 4-bit copy at equal total bits.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10193](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10193), 2024-02 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16863](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16863), 2025-08 (read); [https://arxiv.org/html/2406.08903v3](https://arxiv.org/html/2406.08903v3), 2024-06 (not read directly)

**What does a download-ready quant forget? Feature-level diffing of GGUF, EXL3 and MLX formats, and calibration aimed at the lost features** (LLMs first, then diffusion transformers; self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: At equal bits per weight, GGUF with an importance matrix and EXL3 lose different sets of sparse-autoencoder features (overlap of the lost sets below 0.5), the lost features are predicted by how rarely they fire in the calibration text, and adding top-activating examples of the lost features to the calibration set recovers at least half of them without raising perplexity.  
First experiment: Mac Studio only. Gemma-2-2B with the public Gemma Scope SAEs. Produce Q4_K_M and Q3_K_M GGUF with and without an imatrix (llama.cpp, CPU), and EXL3 at 3.0 and 4.0 bpw; dequantize each back to torch weights. Run 2M tokens of mixed text (wiki, code, three non-English languages) through fp and each …  
Kill criterion: Lost-feature sets overlap above 0.8 across formats at equal bits (formats differ only in amount, not in kind), or feature loss is not predicted by calibration frequency (rank correlation below 0.2), or targeted calibration recovers under 20% of lost features.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03002](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03002), 2026-06 (read); [https://arxiv.org/html/2606.25519v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.25519v1), 2026-06 (read)

**Seconds-per-correct-answer: choosing bits and thinking budget together on memory-bandwidth-bound hardware** (reasoning LLMs; self-rated novelty 2)  
Hypothesis: On an M1 Max at a fixed 12 GB memory budget, the bit width that minimises wall-clock time per correct answer for a thinking model is not the lowest one that fits, because below 4 bits the extra reasoning tokens grow faster than tokens-per-second does.  
First experiment: Mac Studio, llama.cpp Metal, no cloud. Qwen3-4B-Thinking at Q3_K_M, Q4_K_M, Q6_K and Q8_0, plus Qwen3-8B at Q3/Q4 as the bigger-model-fewer-bits arm, all under 12 GB. GSM8K (200 items) and AIME25 (30 items), greedy decoding, thinking budgets of 512, 2k, 8k tokens and unlimited. Record accuracy, …  
Kill criterion: The ranking of configurations by seconds-per-correct-answer matches the ranking by tokens-per-second in every case (inflation never changes the choice), or token inflation at Q3 on these GGUF formats is under 10%, leaving nothing to plan around.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/html/2606.25519v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.25519v1), 2026-06 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04823](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04823), 2025-04 (not read directly)

**Lockstep inference: the smallest set of pinned operations that makes a DiT bit-identical between Apple Metal and NVIDIA CUDA** (image diffusion (applies to any transformer); self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: With packed integer weights and the same kernels on both sides, a full DiT forward pass on an M1 Max and on a Pascal or T4 card diverges at only a handful of operation types (accumulation order, fused multiply-add, and the transcendental functions inside softmax, GELU and the timestep embedding), and pinning just those gives bit-identical images at under 15% slowdown.  
First experiment: Measurement first, cheap. Take one transformer block of Wan 2.1 1.3B with INT8 packed weights and fixed fp32 inputs. Run it through the Metal kernel on the Mac Studio and the CUDA kernel on the GTX 1070-class PC in fp32 (Pascal has no fp16 speed advantage, and fp32 removes one variable; a Modal T4 …  
Kill criterion: Bit identity for a single block needs replacing the platform's attention or matmul with a custom version that is more than 1.5x slower, or the Metal shader compiler cannot be stopped from reordering floating-point math even in its strict math mode.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00279](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00279), 2026-05 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25624](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25624), 2026-09 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17826](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17826), 2025-11 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24904](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24904), 2026-03 (not read directly)

**Nested-bit extrapolation: does pushing a 4-bit model away from its own 3-bit copy move it toward full precision?** (LLMs and diffusion; self-rated novelty 2)  
Hypothesis: When a 3-bit model is mixed with its nested 2-bit copy (the top bits of the same codes), the mixing weight that minimises KL to the full-precision logits is positive, meaning extrapolation away from the coarser copy beats interpolation.  
First experiment: Mac Studio, one afternoon. Gemma-2-2B and Qwen2.5-1.5B with nested uniform quantizers at 4/3 and 3/2 bits. Sweep the mixing weight from -0.5 to +1.0 on logits over 50k tokens and report KL to fp. Repeat on the velocity output of Wan 2.1 1.3B for 16 prompts. Baselines: temperature rescaling alone, …  
Kill criterion: The best mixing weight is zero or negative for both bit pairs on both LLMs and on the DiT, or the gain over temperature rescaling is under 5% of the KL gap. I expect this outcome more likely than not; the write-up would then be a short negative result with …  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14874](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14874), 2024-02 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02507](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02507), 2024-06 (read)

### Lens: humanscale

**The frame-rate cliff: why small neural TTS fails at screen-reader speeds** (speech synthesis (accessibility, CPU and old hardware); self-rated novelty 4)  
Hypothesis: The speech rate at which a small neural TTS model stops being intelligible is set by its acoustic frame rate, so models rank by frame rate (not by size or naturalness score) at 400-900 words per minute, and above the cliff, time-compressing normal-rate output beats the model's own speed control.  
First experiment: All on the Mac Studio CPU, no GPU. Synthesize 200 semantically unpredictable sentences at 200, 300, 450, 600, 750 and 900 wpm with Piper (VITS, about 20-60M), Kokoro (82M), Kitten TTS mini (about 25M), Pocket TTS (about 100M) and eSpeak-ng, each two ways: the model's own speed control, and …  
Kill criterion: Drop it if the cliff rate does not order by frame rate across the four models (rank correlation below 0.5), or if native speed control matches time compression and eSpeak-ng within 5 points of word accuracy at 600 wpm and above, or if the hop-128 retrain does …  
Nearest work listed: [https://stuff.interfree.ca/2026/01/05/ai-tts-for-screenreaders.html](https://stuff.interfree.ca/2026/01/05/ai-tts-for-screenreaders.html), 2026-01-05 (read); [https://gist.github.com/YoraiLevi/cfc6320766905504d23e7100d01e4c7d](https://gist.github.com/YoraiLevi/cfc6320766905504d23e7100d01e4c7d), 2026-08 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06926](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06926), 2025-09 (not read directly)

**Quantization breaks captions by inventing text, not by raising WER** (speech recognition (captioning, atypical speech); self-rated novelty 4)  
Hypothesis: When a Whisper-class model goes from 16-bit to 4-bit weights, its hallucination rate on silence, noise and long pauses rises at least 1.5x while WER on clean speech changes less than 10 percent relative, and the rise is larger for smaller models and for speech with long pauses.  
First experiment: Mac Studio only, whisper.cpp on CPU or Metal. Models: Whisper tiny (39M), base (74M), small (244M), large-v3-turbo (809M) at fp16, q8_0, q5_0, q4_0, plus a 3-bit HQQ variant in PyTorch. Inputs: (a) UrbanSound8K clips, silence and music with no speech; (b) LibriSpeech test-clean with 2-10 s pauses …  
Kill criterion: Drop it if the 4-bit to fp16 hallucination ratio is below 1.2 for every model size with intervals that include 1, meaning hallucination simply tracks WER.  
Nearest work listed: [https://neurips.cc/virtual/2026/156962](https://neurips.cc/virtual/2026/156962), 2026 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08093](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08093), 2025-11 (v2 2026-05) (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12969](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12969), 2025-05 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08021), 2024-02 (not read directly)

**Whose voice does 4-bit erase? Unequal identity loss in quantized voice cloning** (speech synthesis (voice cloning, voice banking); self-rated novelty 3)  
Hypothesis: Weight-only 4-bit quantization of a zero-shot TTS model lowers speaker similarity at least twice as much for voices far from the training distribution (children, elderly, strongly accented, dysarthric) as for typical adult voices, and a small full-precision adapter kept beside the packed weights restores most of it.  
First experiment: Mac Studio for inference; Modal T4 (about 50 free hours a month at the listed price) only if a larger model is added. F5-TTS (about 336M DiT) at fp16 and weight-only 8/6/4-bit using this repo's INT8 ConvRot and GGUF block formats. Clone 50 reference voices per group: VCTK adults, L2-ARCTIC accented …  
Kill criterion: Drop it if the similarity loss ratio between atypical and typical groups stays under 1.25 at every bit width, or the gap vanishes after the real-recording control.

Hypothesis: At least one widely used consumer fp16 path among WebGPU f16 shaders, Vulkan backends, MLX, Core ML on GPU, and CUDA on pre-tensor-core cards accumulates dot products in fp16, and at inner dimension 8192 or more gives relative errors above 1e-2 on realistic rows, while MPS accumulates in fp32.

**The noise floor of paired image metrics: how much of 'quantization distance' is just a different trajectory** (image and video diffusion; self-rated novelty 4)  
Hypothesis: On flow-matching text-to-image models, harmless changes (bf16 instead of fp32, MPS instead of CUDA, weight noise at 0.1% relative size) produce a same-seed LPIPS to the reference that is at least half of what INT8 quantization produces, so most reported 'quantization distance' is trajectory divergence and not damage.

Rungs: same run twice; fp32 vs fp16 vs bf16; MPS vs CUDA; gaussian weight noise at relative size 1e-4, 1e-3, 1e-2; initial-noise nudge of 1e-3; then INT8, W6A8, W4A8, GGUF Q4_K. Small arm: PixArt-Sigma (0.6B) on the GTX 1070/1080-class PC in fp32 (text embeddings precomputed …  
Kill criterion: If the benign rungs (dtype, backend, 0.1% noise) score under 10% of INT8's paired LPIPS on all three models, and paired LPIPS ranks formats the same way preference metrics do, paired metrics are fine as used and this is dropped.

## Round 1 ideas rechecked with real search (round 2)

### Divergence is not damage: a floor and a ceiling for same-seed image metrics (`divergence-vs-damage`)

Hypothesis: Most of the same-seed gap that people report for quantized diffusion models is a harmless switch to a different valid image, not lost quality. Three testable parts: (a) Gaussian noise added to the first-step velocity, matched in norm to a 4-bit model's first-step error, reproduces at least half of that model's same-seed LPIPS gap; (b) changes nobody calls damage (bf16 vs fp32, MPS vs CUDA, 0.1% weight noise) score …

Verdict after search: **partly_open**. Scores (1 to 5): novelty 2, feasibility 4, impact if true 3, chance true 3. Queries run: 73; sources opened: 17.

Prior art found:

- [Albaghdadi, Calibrating a Biomechanical Acceptance Test for Post-Training Quantization of a Video-Diffusion Transformer (Zenodo, record …](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23143508), 2026-10-04. Closest hit to the benign ladder. On one pose-conditioned video DiT (DynamiCtrl, 5.77B) the author builds a null distribution from 57 pairs of numerically equivalent bf16 renders that differ only in attention kernel, a one-unit-in-the-last-place weight dither, or execution platform. Same-seed PSNR against bf16: null median 34.2 …
- [Dongjin Lee, Stepwise Asymmetric Quantization for Flow Matching Models (Zenodo preprint; code at …](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19496643), 2026-04-10. States the candidate's thesis in so many words and backs it with data. FLUX.1-dev, GGUF Q2_K to Q8_0, 25 prompts x 10 fixed seeds, 20 Euler steps, reference Q8_0 at the same seed. A schedule that runs the first 3 steps at Q2_K has worse LPIPS than uniform Q3_K_S (0.233 vs 0.196) but better ImageReward (1.291 vs 1.068, and above …
- [Jin, Evaluation Pitfalls in Post-Training Quantization of Diffusion Transformers: An Empirical Audit (Frontiers in Computing and …](https://doi.org/10.54097/v6881m05), 2026-10-30. New information on the item the file listed as not read: it is now read in full and it does not overlap. It is an FID-only audit on DiT-XL/2 ImageNet 256 (209 records): two sampling-pipeline defects reverse conclusions, a calibration-error proxy has zero rank correlation with FID, and a published grouping trick is …
- [Kaplan, A Calibrated Instrument for Measuring How Inference Optimizations Affect Output Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18005), 2026-09-16. The same instrument on the LLM side: a judge calibrated on two ordinary runs of the same model, a null condition that must read zero, then 4-bit found indistinguishable from 16-bit within about 0.3 points while 3-bit loses 0.5 to 1.1. Shows the null-calibration recipe is already a paper for text models. Not diffusion, not image …
- [Domb, Sackstein, Solberg, HyperQuant (LTX-2 19B video model quantization)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23406), 2026-06-22. Reports same-seed PSNR of 22 to 23 dB against bf16 and explains it as natural divergence of the sample under perturbation, not artefacts. The explanation is asserted and not measured: no floor, no control. One more recent paper leaning on the unmeasured premise. HTML full text searched by keyword.
- [ai-muninn blog, Z-Image Turbo part 2: quality by LPIPS and CLIPScore](https://ai-muninn.com/en/blog/zimage-turbo-quality-lpips-clipscore), 2026-05-04. Community version of the claim on a few-step image model. 6 prompts x 3 seeds on a DGX Spark in ComfyUI. LPIPS against BF16: FP8-scaled 0.167, NVFP4 0.289, NVFP4 plus FP8 text encoder 0.307, with CLIPScore flat at 0.334 to 0.339. Argues LPIPS here measures drift, not damage. No harmless-change baseline of any kind. Read through …
- [vLLM-Omni test file test_quantization_quality.py](https://bezzam-evals-voxtral.hf.space/vllm-omni/tests/diffusion/quantization/test_quantization_quality.py), no date. Same-seed LPIPS against BF16 is used as a pass or fail gate with fixed thresholds (0.15 for images, 0.20 for video). Evidence that uncalibrated same-seed thresholds are now in CI practice, which is the practice the candidate would correct. Seen as a search snippet only.
- [A Trail from Low-Precision Sampling: Diagnosing Super-Diffusive FP32--BF16 Drift in Diffusion Models (ICLR 2027 submission, withdrawn)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=fmZUFWjHbE), 2026-09-17. Title only. By its title it studies how FP32 and BF16 sampling runs drift apart in diffusion models, which is the dtype rung of the benign ladder treated as its own subject. I could not learn whether it uses image metrics or compares the drift with quantization. OpenReview put up a browser challenge, and two web searches found …
- [When Weight Fidelity Misleads: Concept-Erasure Safety Edits Under Diffusion-Model Quantization (ICLR 2027 submission)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=GDTAORcpJ3), 2026-09-17. Title only. Neighbouring claim that closeness of weights does not predict behaviour after quantization, in the safety-edit setting. Probably not a paired-metric calibration paper, but unread.
- [The Hidden Stack: Measuring Inference Backend Effects in Reliable Large Language Model Evaluation (ACL ARR May 2026 submission)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=K7fGsLpaKj), 2026-05-26. Title only. Backend-effect measurement for LLM evaluation. Together with the 92 citers of Yuan 2506.09501 (all LLM or diffusion-LLM by title, for example 2609.26621) it shows the backend and dtype ladder is an active topic for text models and still absent for image diffusion.

What remains open: Two of the idea's three pillars now have a 2026 precedent, both low-visibility Zenodo items. Taken: (1) the claim that same-seed LPIPS mixes harmless divergence with damage, shown with data on FLUX GGUF (Lee, April 2026) and repeated in a blog and in HyperQuant; (2) a null distribution from benign numerical changes (attention kernel, one-ULP weight dither, platform) for same-seed PSNR of a quantized diffusion transformer, with the finding that W8A8 sits only 2.4 to 3.0 dB under a 34.2 dB null (Albaghdadi, October 2026, one video model, artifact without a manuscript); (3) the null-calibrated instrument for LLM quantization (Kaplan, September 2026). Not found anywhere, after about 60 queries and ten citation lists: - A shuffled or otherwise size-matched, structure-destroyed weight-error control on any diffusion model. No hit in OpenAlex, arXiv, OpenReview titles, or the citers of MixDQ, …

Strongest objection: The part that is still new is likely to change no decision, and the parts that would have been the headline are no longer first. Priority: Lee already published the divergence-versus-damage claim with numbers, and Albaghdadi already published a benign null distribution for a quantized diffusion transformer. Albaghdadi's own result cuts against the strong form of the idea: the floor is high, yet W8A8 still separates from it by 2.4 to 3.0 dB on every comparison reported, so same-seed PSNR did detect the 8-bit change once calibrated. The shuffled control: rounding error without clipping is …

Revised first experiment: One model, two stages, Mac Studio only for stage 1. Setup: Krea 2 Turbo, 512 squared, 8 steps, euler, noise drawn on CPU, text conditioning cached once and shared by all arms. Metric: same-seed LPIPS (AlexNet) and PSNR against the bf16 reference, median over images, bootstrap 95% interval over prompts. Stage 1, go or no-go, 30 prompts x 2 seeds = 60 images per arm, 7 arms, about 8 hours on the Mac Studio (stock path about 68 s per image, Metal path about 30 s): 1. bf16 reference. 2. Rerun of the reference in a fresh process (must be bit-identical; if not, that is the true floor). 3. fp16 …

### Is quantization error in a sampler a repeatable bias or fresh noise? One number, two fixes (`bias-or-noise`)

Hypothesis: Along the fp16 trajectory, the velocity error caused by quantized weights at one step points in nearly the same direction as at the next step (cosine rho above 0.5 for many-step models). If so, two training-free fixes follow: one full-precision anchor step used to subtract the error removes at least 30% of final-latent error, and alternating floor and ceil 4-bit roundings of 5-bit codes across steps shrinks …

Verdict after search: **partly_open**. Scores (1 to 5): novelty 3, feasibility 4, impact if true 2, chance true 3. Queries run: 48; sources opened: 19.

Prior art found:

- [ASSERT (Feng et al., ASP-DAC 2027): stochastic sampling against analog compute-in-memory weight noise in diffusion models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00955), 2026-09-01. Closest hit, and it takes the framing. It says in plain terms that a fixed weight perturbation reused at every sampling step gives per-step errors that are correlated, not fresh draws. It writes the first-order recursion for the error, splits the final error energy into per-step terms plus cross-step inner products (its Eq. …
- [QuAKE: Quantization-Aware Kalman Estimation for Diffusion Sampling (Shi, Jin, Zhang, Gu)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21407), 2026-09-18. Newest and strongest sampler-side fix that uses the history of quantized outputs. A Kalman filter with a smooth-trajectory prior and a calibrated per-timestep, per-channel mean for the quantization error. It assumes the leftover error is independent from step to step. W4A4 text-to-image models including FLUX.1-dev at 20 steps …
- [PulseQuant: 4-bit post-training quantization for video diffusion transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33384), 2026-09-29. Overlaps the closed-loop part on the same model family. 80 isolated 4-bit interventions (one block at one step) on Wan 2.1-14B, each followed by a full continuation. Local block-output error barely predicts final latent error (rho = -0.08); error propagated four steps does (rho = 0.70). Evaluated on Wan 2.1 1.3B and 14B at …
- [Closing the Null Space (GAMP): CFG-aware post-training quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08241), 2026-07-09. Touches only the side question about conditional versus unconditional error under guidance. It shows the unconditional branch can drift while the cond-minus-uncond gap looks fine. No cross-step measurement.
- [CentriQ: calibration-free low-bit quantization for diffusion transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06260), 2026-10-05. Has a weight-only table (W4A16, W3A16; RTN, NF4, HQQ on PixArt-Sigma, FLUX.1-schnell, SANA-1.6B) but only final FID and ImageReward. No per-step error analysis. Keyword-scanned, not read end to end.
- [Absorbing Quantization Error by Deformable Noise Scheduler (ICML 2026)](https://icml.cc/virtual/2026/poster/64968), 2026. Treats quantization error as a shift of the effective timestep at the level of the whole distribution and extends to flow matching. That is a population-level, per-step view: nothing per-sample and nothing on cross-step correlation. Poster summary only; full text not found.
- [Q-Drift (already in the file; new detail from the raw text)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18095), 2026-03. New detail only: the text states that its model uses independent perturbations across steps. Together with QuAKE and TCEC that makes three 2026 sampler-side methods built on step-to-step independence, none of which checks it. Also now listed as a NeurIPS 2026 workshop paper on OpenReview.
- [TMPDiff (already in the file; new detail from the raw text)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14062), 2026-03-14. New detail only: its additivity check correlates scalar final-error changes from single-step precision toggles (Pearson and rank correlations of magnitudes). It never compares the direction of errors injected at different steps, so it does not answer the cosine question. PixArt-Sigma and Sana are the models used for that check.

What remains open: Still unpublished, as far as I could find in about 45 queries and 19 opened sources: 1. The measured step-by-step cosine (or Gram) matrix of the velocity error caused by weight quantization, per sample, on any flow-matching transformer. ASSERT (Sept 2026) names this measurement and leaves it for future work, and it studied analog noise on old U-Nets, not quantization. 2. The three-way split of error energy into population mean per step, per-sample part that repeats across steps, and fresh per-step part. No paper reports it for any model. 3. Any of this for weight-only formats people actually run (GGUF Q4_K, packed 4-bit). The published sampler-side work is W4A4 with calibration. 4. A direct test of the step-to-step independence assumed by Q-Drift, QuAKE and TCEC: how much error is left, and how correlated it is across steps, after removing the calibrated per-timestep per-channel mean. …

Strongest objection: The headline result is close to a foregone conclusion and its framing is already in print. The same wrong weights applied to a slowly changing input must give similar errors on neighbouring steps, and ASSERT published that argument in September 2026. So a high cross-step cosine alone is a confirmation, not a finding. The only parts that could surprise are the size of the per-sample share versus the population mean, and whether it can be used. On use there are two hard problems. First, a per-sample fix needs a full-precision reference for that sample, which the memory-limited user does not …

Revised first experiment: Open-loop measurement only; no fix is built until it passes. Models: (A) Wan 2.1 1.3B, 25 steps, guidance on, 480x832, 17 frames. (B) one 20-step image model that TMPDiff used, PixArt-Sigma or Sana. (C) one 4-step model whose fp16 reference fits in 32 GB, SDXL-Turbo first choice. Formats: the repo's packed 4-bit weight quantizer and GGUF Q4_K, both applied as dequantized fp16 weights so only the weight error differs; 8-bit as the floor. Data: 16 prompts x 4 seeds = 64 samples per model. Method: run the fp16 model once and save its input at every step. Replay those saved inputs through the …

### How many weight bits does the backward pass need? A rate curve for LoRA gradients (`backward-bits`)

Hypothesis: With an exact INT8 forward pass, a backward pass that uses only the top 2 bit planes of each frozen weight gives LoRA gradients with cosine at least 0.9 to the full-precision gradient and the same held-out loss within seed spread. Sign-only with a row scale degrades clearly, and random feedback weights fail. The curve of gradient quality against backward bits is the result.

No recheck file.

### The slow axis in autoregressive speech: training-free layer reuse between adjacent frames (`speech-slow-axis`)

Hypothesis: In autoregressive speech models, the residual update of middle layers at one audio frame is nearly the same as at the previous frame (cosine above 0.9), and clearly more so than in a text model run on text. Reusing or linearly forecasting those updates, with a full refresh every few frames, skips at least 30% of layer evaluations while WER stays within +0.3 absolute and speaker similarity within -0.01.

Verdict after search: **partly_open**. Scores (1 to 5): novelty 3, feasibility 4, impact if true 2, chance true 2. Queries run: 52; sources opened: 33.

- [SPADE: Structured Pruning and Adaptive Distillation for Efficient LLM-TTS (ICASSP 2026)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20802), 2025-09-25. Layer-redundancy measurement in speech-INPUT LLMs (ASR and speech translation), by pruning. Finds the redundancy is inherited from the text LLM and that speech and text inputs give similar redundant blocks. It is the understanding side, not speech generation, and it compares layers, not adjacent frames. It weakens the 'speech …
- [Interpreting and Steering a Text-to-Speech Language Model with Sparse Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10029), 2026-06-08. The closest work that actually looks inside the residual stream of an AR TTS LM (CosyVoice 3). Sparse autoencoder features for phonemes, laughter, speaker gender, with steering. I searched the full text for cosine, adjacent, consecutive, neighbouring, frame-to-frame, persistence, temporal, autocorrelation: nothing. No …
- [Discovering Interpretable Acoustic Features in Autoregressive Voice-Cloning TTS Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40505), 2026-10-29. SAE, PCA and probing study on Qwen3-TTS plus five other AR TTS models. Full text searched for the same terms. Only a side remark that steering compounds over time and pitch seems encoded locally. No measurement of how much the per-layer update changes from frame to frame, no reuse.
- [Speech Block Influence: Component-Specific Layer Scoring for Pruning Speech LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29343), 2026-09-24. Layer-importance scoring for pruning speech-input LLMs. Scores decoder layers on text positions only because audio positions dominate the input-output similarity. Abstract only. Static pruning, understanding side, not frame-axis.
- [FlexiSLM: A Spoken Language Model with Dynamic and Controllable Frame Rates (EMNLP 2026)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31247), 2026-06-30. Attacks the same redundancy from the input side: merges adjacent frames whose codec features are similar, down to about 4 Hz, with training. Abstract only. If frames that are alike get merged before the LM ever sees them, the frame-to-frame slack that layer reuse would harvest is already gone.
- [Hearing's Blind Spots: Toward Perceptual Compute Allocation in Neural TTS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14056), 2026-10-06. The only paper citing SPAR-K (Semantic Scholar citation graph). Abstract read. It studies where spectral damage is audible in Piper/Kokoro output. No LM layers, no skipping. Not the target.
- [vSkipper (serving engine for dynamic layer-skipping text LLMs)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37062), 2026-09-29. The only paper citing LoRA-Drop. Abstract read. A serving layer for per-token layer skippers in text LLMs. No speech. Relevant only as evidence that batched serving of per-token skipping needs special engine work, which is the KV-cache-hole problem the candidate would also hit.
- [Decode Before You Hear: Speculative Listening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06982), 2026-11-04. Training-free, lossless self-speculative decoding for full-duplex spoken LMs. Abstract only. Different mechanism (speculate and verify, not layer reuse), but it is a training-free speed-up on speech LMs published this month, so the 'training-free speech LM acceleration' slot is being filled from another side.
- [DELTA-TTS: Adapting Autoregressive Model into Diffusion Language Model for Text-to-Speech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04140), 2026-07-05. LoRA adaptation that turns a pretrained AR TTS LM into a discrete diffusion LM; a web summary says about 3.3x faster token generation (I read the abstract only and did not verify that number). Trained. Another larger, trained speed-up that the training-free ceiling has to be compared with.
- [T^2MLR: Transformer with Temporal Middle-Layer Recurrence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15178), 2026-07-16. Text LLMs: feeds the cached middle-layer state of the previous token into an earlier layer of the current token, with fine-tuning. Abstract only. Same 'carry the middle block across time' geometry as the candidate, but used to add reasoning capacity, not to skip compute, and not on speech.
- [Anatomy of the Modality Gap: Dissecting the Internal States of End-to-End Speech LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01502), 2026-03. Layer-wise CKA of speech versus text inside speech-input LLMs. Abstract only. Attributes the broad speech alignment band to one semantic unit spanning several frames. Cross-modal alignment, not adjacent-frame update similarity, not generation.
- [SPAR-K (already in the file; new information only)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09215), 2026-03. New fact from a direct full-text search: SPAR-K contains no hidden-state or update similarity measurement between adjacent speech frames. The claim that speech tokens are locally predictable is asserted with citations to codec papers, not measured inside the LM. So the measurement gap in the closest known paper is real.
- [LoRA-Drop (already in the file; new information only)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02569), 2026-01. New fact from a direct full-text search: the adjacent-token hidden-state cosine it reports is text only (about 0.8 to 0.95 at distance 1) and the paper never mentions speech or audio. So the text baseline the speech number must beat is already high.
- [ReFrame (already in the file; new information only)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01835), 2026-11. New fact from a direct full-text search: its statement about similar hidden states in neighbouring frames is about silence frames, and it uses a hidden-state cosine threshold as a verification gate. It is not a per-layer measurement across normal speech and has no text comparison.

What remains open: I found no paper, preprint, OpenReview submission or GitHub repo from 2024 to 9 November 2026 that does either half of the candidate. Still unpublished, as far as this search reached: 1. The measurement itself. Nobody reports, per layer, how similar the residual update at speech frame t is to the update at frame t-1 inside an autoregressive TTS language model, with the mean removed, and nobody compares that with the same backbone running on text (CosyVoice 2 LM against Qwen2.5-0.5B, Llasa-1B against Llama-3.2-1B). SPAR-K asserts local predictability without measuring it in the LM. LoRA-Drop measures text only. ReFrame's remark covers silence. The two SAE papers on TTS LMs look at features, not at frame-to-frame change. 2. The functional version of the measurement: replacing a block's update with the previous frame's update, and showing this beats a constant per-layer mean and a far-away …

Strongest objection: The measurement is open mostly because the answer has little value either way, and the new prior art makes that worse. First, the speed ceiling is small and already beaten. Reusing a 30% middle block on every other frame saves 15% of layer compute, about 1.18x at best; reusing it on every frame would be 1.43x but cannot hold quality. SPADE already gets 1.41x on Llasa and up to 1.7x on CosyVoice 2 by dropping half the layers once, with a short distillation run, and it needs no special KV-cache handling. A reused layer writes no fresh keys and values for that frame, so later frames attend to …

Hypothesis: Take one backbone used for two jobs: Orpheus 3B (speech tokens) and its Llama 3B text parent. At each GGUF level from Q8_0 down to Q2_K, the rate at which the quantized model's top token differs from fp16, plotted against the fp16 output entropy at that position, falls on one curve for both models. Speech, which samples from higher-entropy distributions, stays usable at lower bits than text. One fitted temperature …

### The Muon quantization paradox: the optimizer decides which kind of quantization a model survives (`muon-paradox`)

Hypothesis: At matched validation loss, a Muon-trained transformer loses less than an AdamW-trained one under weight-only 4-bit quantization and more under activation-only 4-bit quantization (the sign of the gap flips). The stable rank of the weight matrices predicts the activation-side loss across layers and runs.

Verdict after search: **partly_open**. Scores (1 to 5): novelty 3, feasibility 4, impact if true 3, chance true 1. Queries run: 34; sources opened: 28.

What is no longer open: the weight-only comparison at small scale with seeds (SOMA did it, and …

Strongest objection: The sign flip has no data behind it and now has three pieces of evidence against it. First, the weight-only half: SOMA (October 2026), with 3 training seeds and 3 calibration seeds, finds Muon no better than AdamW under 4-bit weight-only quantization on nanoGPT and Pythia-70M (+3.98% vs +3.55%, and +10.96% vs +11.12%). The predicted Muon advantage is not there at the scale the candidate wanted to train at. Second, the activation half: Park et al. Table 2 already shows Adam, not Muon, is hurt more when activations go to 4 bits, and S2D-style reasoning says flatter weight spectra mean fewer …

Revised first experiment: Step 0 only, inference-only, on the Mac. Do not train anything until it says yes. Models: the three public Park et al. checkpoints, dmis-lab/OSP-1.4B-100B-Adam, -Muon and the Muon-Only variant. Adam vs Muon-Only is the clean optimizer pair (no architecture changes). I did not verify they load on Apple GPU; fp32 size is about 5.6 GB each, so memory is fine on 32 GB. Data: WikiText-2 test, cut to about 100k tokens, split into 3 disjoint thirds to get a noise floor. No calibration data (round-to-nearest only). Grid: weight bits {16, 6, 4, 3} x activation bits {16, 8, 6, 4}. Two quantizers so the …

### EMA length as a free quantization dial (`ema-quant-dial`)

Hypothesis: For EDM2 models, the EMA length that gives the best 4-bit model is longer than the EMA length that gives the best full-precision model, by more than one step on the published grid. Picking the EMA for the target bit width recovers a measurable share of the 4-bit loss at zero cost.

No recheck file.

### Quantization breaks captions by inventing text, not by raising WER (`whisper-invented-text`)

Hypothesis: When Whisper is quantized, the rate of invented text on non-speech audio and on long pauses rises by at least 1.5x at a bit width where WER on clean speech moves by under 10% relative. Keeping only the three decoder heads that Calm-Whisper identified at 8 bits removes most of the rise.

Verdict after search: **partly_open**. Scores (1 to 5): novelty 3, feasibility 4, impact if true 2, chance true 2. Queries run: 65; sources opened: 31.

Prior art found:

- [Do LLM Decoders Listen Fairly? (Ginjala et al., same group as Temporal Taxation)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21276), 2026-04-23. Closest new hit and the main scooping risk. Full precision only. It injects silence into utterances (25, 50, 75 percent of utterance duration at a random position) as one of 12 acoustic degradations, across 9 models including Whisper small, medium and large-v3, and classifies the inserted words (repetition, syntactic, content). …
- [WhisperKit: On-device Real-time ASR with Billion-Scale Transformers (Argmax)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.10860), 2025-07. States in the text, without data, that naive compression of Whisper raises WER and also brings hallucination and repeated patterns. Reports only WER for its own compression (OD-MBP keeps WER within about 1 percent while shrinking large-v3-turbo from 1.6 GB to 0.6 GB). So the direction of the candidate claim is already asserted …
- [WhisperKit 'Quality of Inference' (QoI) metric in the whisperkit-coreml model card and whisperkittools](https://huggingface.co/argmaxinc/whisperkit-coreml), no date. An existing shipped metric built on the same idea that average WER hides damage from compression: the share of test examples where the compressed model is no worse than the float16 reference. It is computed on speech test sets only, so it does not cover silence or non-speech. I read the whisperkittools README and the …
- [GHOST-Q: Grounding Hallucinations Overlooked Under Same-score TradeOffs in Quantized VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29999), 2026-09-24. Same shape of claim in a different modality: hallucination shifts under quantization while the headline score stays the same, for vision-language models. Shows the framing is not new. I downloaded it and checked the title and term counts only (19 mentions of hallucination, 44 of quantization); I did not read the body.
- [Efficient Compression of Multitask Multilingual Speech Models (Ferraz, 2024)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00966), 2024-05. Cited by Temporal Taxation as the earlier study of 8-bit Whisper quantization and bias on FLEURS and Common Voice. Full text has zero mentions of hallucination, silence or insertion. Confirms the pre-2026 quantization literature never looked at this failure mode.
- [Pruning for Efficiency, Paying in Fairness: Demographic Disparities in Pruned Speech-LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38106), 2026-09-29. A citer of the silence-injection paper and the nearest sign that the fairness-under-compression line is being extended fast. It covers pruning of speech LLMs. Full text has zero mentions of hallucination or silence.
- [whisper.cpp issue #2454: tests: add WER benchmarks](https://github.com/ggml-org/whisper.cpp/issues/2454), 2024-10-05. The project's own request for regression testing of builds asks for WER only. No hallucination or non-speech check is proposed there or in any other issue or discussion I could find in whisper.cpp, faster-whisper, openai/whisper or mlx-examples. This is the natural home for a non-speech insertion check if the effect is real.
- [DiffAQ (already in the file; new detail from the full text)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22395), 2026-10. New detail: it says standard GPTQ and AWQ on ASR models fall into complete hallucination loops at 2 and 3 bits, and notes that zero-padded and static frames dominate calibration data. Its tables are WER only on LibriSpeech and FLEURS. No non-speech test, and nothing at the 4 to 8 bit widths that people actually ship.
- [TARQ (already in the file; new detail from the full text)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27808), 2026-05. New detail: it uses whisper.cpp ggml Q4_0 only for latency rows. Zero mentions of hallucination or silence in the full text. It does not measure the target.
- [How to Reduce Whisper Hallucination (plus ten other 2025 to 2026 Whisper hallucination papers checked the same way)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32560), 2026-09-26. Negative evidence. I grepped the full text of eleven recent hallucination papers for quantiz, int8, q5_0, q4_0, gguf, ggml, whisper.cpp, ctranslate and faster-whisper: 2609.32560, 2604.08591, 2609.04404, 2608.15940, 2606.23048, 2609.04561, 2606.07473, 2511.14219, 2502.12414, 2510.16567, 2606.23060. Zero hits in all of them. The …

What remains open: I found no paper, issue or discussion that measures how often Whisper invents text on non-speech audio or long pauses as a function of the quantized format people actually download (whisper.cpp q8_0, q5_0, q4_0; CTranslate2 / faster-whisper int8; WhisperKit and MLX low-bit builds). Three specific things are still unpublished as far as this search can show: (1) the rate itself, per format, on non-speech clips and on speech with long inserted pauses, at bit widths where clean-speech WER is flat; (2) the sign of the effect, since nobody has shown whether quantization makes silence hallucination worse, better, or leaves it alone; (3) which part is responsible (encoder versus decoder, and whether Whisper's own no-speech and log-probability thresholds or a VAD front end erase the effect). What is not open: the general idea that average WER hides quantization damage (Temporal Taxation, TARQ, …

Strongest objection: The one group best placed to publish this is one experiment away. Ginjala and co-authors already have a silence-injection harness with insertion classification (April 2026) and a Whisper compression sweep (September 2026), and their own paper points out that the two do not share a cell. Filling that cell is the obvious next paper for them, and they have more compute and a fairness angle that reviewers like. Second, the result may be empty or boring: Temporal Taxation found INT8 to be gentle (7 significant changes out of 427) and found nothing significant at large-v3, so an effect at shipped …

Revised first experiment: Runs on the Mac Studio M1 Max, CPU and Metal only, zero dollars. Step 0 (about 3 to 4 hours, PyTorch on CPU, go or no-go): take whisper base and small, fake-quantize the weights to 8, 5 and 4 bits with the same block-wise scheme whisper.cpp uses, run one decoder step on 500 UrbanSound8K clips and 500 LibriSpeech test-clean utterances, and count how often the first-token decision flips between 'no speech / end of text' and a word token. Go if at least 10 percent of non-speech clips flip while under 1 percent of speech first tokens change. Stop if under 3 percent flip. Step 1 (my estimate: 1 to …

### Below 4 bits on GPUs without matrix units: put the rate allocation in the layout, and measure the decode-ops law (`sub4bit-layout-decode-law`)

Hypothesis: On GPUs with no integer matrix units, throughput of a packed GEMM is set by decode operations per weight, not by bytes read. So a format that gives each column its own fixed bit width (a staircase chosen by water-filling on sensitivity) reaches 3.0 bits per weight at better perplexity than uniform 3-bit, while running at 80% or more of the nibble kernel's speed. A fused entropy decoder (rANS or Rice) saves more bits …

No recheck file.

### Rank-space GEMM and the merging gap: how low-rank are DiT token matrices really? (`rank-space-gemm`)

Hypothesis: The inputs to the large linear layers in DiTs (qkv, attention output, MLP up-projection) can be written with rank under a quarter of min(tokens, width) at 3% error, across blocks and steps. Token merging, which is a crude fixed-basis version of this, leaves a gap of more than 10% in error against the best rank-r approximation at equal rank. A randomized range finder then gives at least 1.2x end-to-end on the linears.

No recheck file.

### The frame-rate cliff: why small neural TTS fails at screen-reader speeds (`tts-frame-rate-cliff`)

Hypothesis: Each neural TTS model has a speaking rate at which intelligibility collapses, and that rate orders by the model's acoustic frame rate. Time-compressing audio made at normal rate stays intelligible further than the model's own speed control. Retraining one small VITS with hop 128 instead of hop 256 moves the cliff to a higher rate.

No recheck file.

### A fill-fraction law for the 4-bit cliff (`fill-fraction-law`)

Hypothesis: Damage from post-training quantization collapses onto one curve when plotted against fill fraction: bits the model has stored, divided by (bit width times parameter count). Models of different sizes with the same fill fraction lose the same amount at the same bit width; small models break at 4 bits because they are full, not because they are small.

No recheck file.

### Does an adapter learn the quantizer's errors? White-error test, portability, and dither (`adapter-learns-quantizer`)

Hypothesis: On an unrotated 4-bit base, a rank-32 side branch can remove more than 20% of the activation-weighted weight error, but under 5% after a Hadamard rotation. A LoRA trained on the unrotated quantized base therefore drifts toward W minus W_q as training goes on, and its quality gap when moved to the full-precision base grows with training length. Rotation, or re-drawing the rounding each training step, halves that gap.

No recheck file.

## Round 2: eight new lenses, 48 ideas

### Lens: anomalies

**Do image generators learn subliminally? A fine-tune leaking through pictures that do not show it** (image generation (diffusion and flow models); a safety and …; self-rated novelty 4, ambition 4)  
Hypothesis: A student image model that starts from the same base weights as a fine-tuned teacher picks up the teacher's hidden concept (for example one person's face) when it is fine-tuned only on teacher images that contain no faces at all, and a student started from a different base does not.  
First experiment: SD 1.5 (860M UNet) on the GTX 1070 in fp32 or a free T4. (1) Teacher: train an identity LoRA with no trigger word so that 'a photo of a person' always gives face X (about 1 hour). (2) Carrier set: 4,000 teacher images of prompts with no people (landscapes, objects, textures), 20 steps each; drop …  
Kill criterion: Same-base student's face similarity to X is not above the control student's beyond seed spread (3 seeds, gap under 0.02 ArcFace cosine) at 4,000 carrier images, and the weight-change cosine is not above the control's. Then the image-sample channel is closed …  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14805](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14805), 2025-07 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08538](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08538), 2026-11 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10657](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10657), 2026-10 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38680](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38680), 2026-09 (not read directly)

**Removable scaffolds: give a model free hints during training, take them away at the end** (LLM training method (small language models from scratch); …; self-rated novelty 3, ambition 4)  
Hypothesis: A small language model trained with extra attention hints that are computed for free from the input (previous token, last place this same token appeared) and with those hints removed for the final 15% of training reaches the baseline's final loss with at least 25% fewer training tokens.  
First experiment: M1 Max, PyTorch MPS. A 30M-parameter GPT (8 layers, width 512, context 512) on a 200M-token slice of OpenWebText or TinyStories. Four arms, 3 seeds each: baseline with rotary positions; rotary dropped for the last 15% (a small DroPE replication); attention-bias hints for 85% then removed; hints …  
Kill criterion: The hint-then-remove arm does not reach the baseline's final loss at least 10% earlier in tokens across 3 seeds, or its loss after removal ends more than 0.01 nats worse than baseline.  
Nearest work listed: [https://pub.sakana.ai/DroPE/](https://pub.sakana.ai/DroPE/), 2026-01 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.13571](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.13571), 2023-05 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13129](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13129), 2025-02 (not read directly)

**Position, not error build-up, is why autoregressive speech models fall apart on long text** (speech (autoregressive codec-token TTS); self-rated novelty 3, ambition 3)  
Hypothesis: A codec-token TTS model trained on utterances up to 15 seconds, with its rotary positions dropped for the last 15% of training, keeps word error rate at 60-120 seconds within 2x of its short-utterance value, while its twin that keeps rotary positions gets more than 5x worse.  
First experiment: M1 Max, from scratch. A 50M-parameter decoder-only model over a single-codebook 40-50 token/s public codec, character or phoneme input, LibriTTS-R clean subsets (about 190 hours), utterances cut at 15 s. Three arms, 2 seeds: rotary throughout; no positions from the start; rotary then dropped for …  
Kill criterion: At 60 s the dropped-position arm's word error rate is not at least 2x lower than the rotary twin's, or its error on 15 s utterances is more than 20% worse (relative). Either one ends it.  
Nearest work listed: [https://pub.sakana.ai/DroPE/](https://pub.sakana.ai/DroPE/), 2026-01 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22179](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22179), 2024-10 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18603](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18603), 2024-12 (not read directly)

**A distilled model is a fixed-gain amplifier: a rule for LoRA strength on Turbo and Lightning models** (image and video generation (how guidance-distilled models …; self-rated novelty 4, ambition 2)  
Hypothesis: The LoRA multiplier needed on a guidance-distilled model to match the LoRA's effect on its guided teacher equals 1 + (w - 1)(1 - r), where w is the teacher's guidance scale and r is how much of the LoRA's effect also shows on the unconditional pass, so trigger-word LoRAs need about w times the strength and global style LoRAs about 1 time.  
First experiment: GTX 1070 or free T4, SD 1.5 with a public LCM or Hyper-SD distilled checkpoint as student and the base at guidance 7.5 as teacher. Twelve public LoRAs: six trigger-word characters, six global styles. For each, measure r directly from single forward passes at four noise levels (cheap), then find the …  
Kill criterion: Across the twelve LoRAs the measured multiplier does not track the predicted one (rank correlation under 0.5), or trigger-word and style LoRAs need the same multiplier.  
Nearest work listed: [https://reddit.sentinel-team.org](https://reddit.sentinel-team.org), 2026-01 (read); [https://www.runcomfy.com/trainer/ai-toolkit/z-image-base-vs-turbo-lora-training](https://www.runcomfy.com/trainer/ai-toolkit/z-image-base-vs-turbo-lora-training) (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07274](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07274), 2025-03 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.05556](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.05556), 2023-11 (not read directly)

**A flow model that reads the noise level from its input corrects its own sampling mistakes** (image generation (flow matching; small models from scratch, …; self-rated novelty 2, ambition 3)  
Hypothesis: A velocity-prediction flow model whose time input is removed after training and briefly recalibrated matches the original at the standard schedule and loses less quality than the original when the true noise level of the sample differs from what the sampler assumes (few steps, noise injected mid-way, image-to-image at the wrong strength).  
First experiment: Stage A, M1 Max, about 1 day: CIFAR-10, 35M-parameter flow model, three arms: with time input; without from scratch; with, then removed for the last 15%. About 4 hours each. Measure FID-10k at 4, 8, 16 and 50 Euler steps; after injecting extra noise (std 0.1-0.3) at the half-way point; and for …  
Kill criterion: After recalibration within 15% of the training budget the model is more than 10% worse in FID at the standard schedule, or under all three mismatch tests its quality loss is not smaller than the original's (ratio of FID increases 0.9 or above).  
Nearest work listed: [https://intuitivepapers.ai/geometry-of-noise/](https://intuitivepapers.ai/geometry-of-noise/), 2026-06 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13129](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13129), 2025-02 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20416](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20416), 2026-06 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25289](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25289), 2026-04 (not read directly)

**The missing control in layer repetition: is it iteration, or just turning up the volume?** (LLM (training-free, public models up to about 30B in 4-bit …; self-rated novelty 2, ambition 2)  
Hypothesis: Most of the benchmark change from repeating a middle block of a frozen LLM, both the gains on reasoning probes and the losses on instruction following, is reproduced by multiplying that block's update by a constant with no extra compute.  
First experiment: M1 Max with MLX. Qwen3-4B, Llama-3.2-3B, Qwen3-8B, plus one 27-32B model in 4-bit. Five arms on the same middle block (4 layers by the published rule, and a 6-7 layer RYS-style block): baseline; naive repeat; update scaled by 1.25, 1.5 and 2 with no extra pass; re-evaluation with total update size …  
Kill criterion: The best scaling-only arm reproduces under 30% of the naive-repeat effect on both the gaining tasks and the losing tasks in at least 3 of 4 models. Then repetition really is iteration, which is itself worth stating with the control included.  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23872](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23872), 2026-05 (read); [https://dnhkng.github.io/posts/rys/](https://dnhkng.github.io/posts/rys/), 2026-03 (read); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00716](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00716), 2026-04 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17981](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17981), 2026-08 (not read directly)

**Image tokens are read before the question is known: a second look at the image after the question** (vision-language models (training-free, public 0.5B-7B …; self-rated novelty 3, ambition 2)  
Hypothesis: Placing a second copy of the image tokens after the question improves fine-detail visual question answering on frozen VLMs by more than placing the question first or repeating the question, and repeating only the patches the question attends to keeps most of that gain at under 20% extra tokens.  
First experiment: M1 Max with MLX-VLM, about 1 day. Three public models (for example a 0.5B LLaVA-OneVision or SmolVLM2, Qwen2.5-VL-3B and Qwen2.5-VL-7B in 4-bit). Five layouts: image then question (standard); question then image; image, question, image; question, image, question; image, question, then only the top …  
Kill criterion: Image-question-image does not beat the standard layout by at least 1.5 points averaged over the detail-heavy sets on at least 2 of 3 models, or question-then-image alone gives the same gain (then it is only an ordering effect and already folklore).  
Nearest work listed: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.14982](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.14982), 2025-12 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01792](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01792), 2026-07 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18620](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18620), 2024-11 (not read directly); [https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07580](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07580), 2025-12 (not read directly)

### Lens: arch

**Surprise feedback: tell the model how wrong it just was (LONG SHOT)** (text LM first; applies to any autoregressive model (audio, …; self-rated novelty 4, ambition 4)  
Hypothesis: A causal transformer that receives, at each position, an embedding of how surprised its own mid-layer exit head was by the token that just arrived reaches lower loss at equal training FLOPs than a control with the same extra parameters and the same auxiliary exit loss but no feedback.  
First experiment: Stage 1, M1 Max, about one day. Models with 4 layers, width 128 to 256 (1M to 3M parameters), exit at layer 2, 3 seeds per arm. Tasks: switching in-context Markov chains over 64 symbols (the chain changes at a random point) and in-context linear regression. Arms: (i) own mid-layer surprisal and …  
Kill criterion: Stage 1: arm (i) is not better than arm (iii) beyond seed spread on post-switch excess loss. Stage 2: the gain is under 0.01 nats at the end of training, or it shrinks steadily toward zero along the curve, which would mean the model learns the signal on its …

**Predictive-coding depth: spend upper layers only where the model was surprised (LONG SHOT)** (text LM first; the larger payoff would be audio and video …; self-rated novelty 3, ambition 4)  
Hypothesis: When only half of the positions pass through the upper half of the layers, choosing them by the surprisal of the arriving token under a mid-layer exit head gives lower loss than a learned Mixture-of-Depths router and lower loss than an equal-FLOP uniformly smaller model.  
First experiment: Step 1, training-free, about one day on the M1 Max. On public checkpoints (GPT-2 small, medium, large; Pythia 160M to 1.4B; one further public model of 0.5B to 1.5B), fit a linear exit head at mid depth on about 2M tokens. Then skip the upper layers at a fraction f of positions chosen by (a) lowest …  
Kill criterion: Step 1: the surprisal rule is not clearly below random at f of 30 to 50 percent in two of the three model families. Step 2: the surprisal rule does not beat the learned MoD router by 0.01 nats, or does not beat the equal-FLOP dense model.

**Cross-fitted distillation: soft targets only from a teacher that never saw the document** (text LM; applies to any fixed-data training (speech, …; self-rated novelty 3, ambition 3)  
Hypothesis: In multi-epoch training on a fixed corpus, a student distilled from teachers that did not train on the document being labelled reaches lower validation loss, at equal total compute, than a student distilled from teachers that did train on it.

**Train with a lookup table, ship without it** (text LM; self-rated novelty 3, ambition 3)  
Hypothesis: A transformer trained with a hashed n-gram lookup table whose gate is annealed to zero over the last third of training ends with lower validation loss, at equal backbone FLOPs and with no table, than the same transformer trained without a table.

**Loop or look up: one variable that predicts which tricks transfer** (text LM; a finding about training regimes; self-rated novelty 3, ambition 3)  
Hypothesis: Whether a lookup table (parameters without FLOPs) or a repeated block (FLOPs without parameters) lowers validation loss at fixed training compute is predicted by the number of passes over the data, with tables winning below about one to two passes and loops winning above.

## Round 3: twenty-seven lenses, 195 ideas

### Lens: backwards

**Same 3.28, different models: split the speedrun's loss by how new the text is** (language / evaluation; wildness 2)  
Claim: Two models that reach the same validation loss on FineWeb are not equally good. As the speedrun records got faster, the loss on text that has no long exact match in the training set got worse while the loss on near-copied text got better, and the single number hid it. The recent lookup-table records are the extreme case of a drift that started earlier.  
Prediction: At matched validation loss 3.28: (a) hint-era runs (PR 367/380 style) are at least 0.15 nats worse than a mid-2025 record on validation tokens with no 6-token match in training text, and at least 0.20 nats worse on non-FineWeb text (for example code or a book). (b) Among pre-hint records, the …  
Control that could embarrass it: Train the oldest record longer or shorter so that its total loss differs by the same amount as the seed-to-seed spread, and check the no-match loss moves with it. If the split loss is just a rescaled copy of the total loss across all records (correlation …  
Kill criterion: If the no-match loss across pre-hint records varies by less than 0.01 nats, or less than twice the seed spread, part (b) is dead. Part (a) alone is then only a confirmation of what the PR commenters already suspect and is not worth writing up.  
Cost: About 10 short GPU runs (under 2 GPU-hours total on a rented H100 or equivalent; the old records were written for 8 GPUs so wall time on one is longer, maybe 30 to 45 min each for the oldest). CPU …
